We want: for t : ∀X. X→X, closed, U wf, v : U value, (t[U]) v -->* v.

Standard parametricity (Reynolds): take the binary relation R on U-values defined as "both components are v" (or equality). Then t[U] relates to t[U], and v R v, so (t[U]) v reduces to a value related to v, i.e., equals v. But with nondeterminism, multi-step exists: logical relation must require existence of reduction paths producing related values. Let me design the logical relation carefully, including nondeterminism.

Standard approach in these benchmark tasks (System F parametricity "theorem for free"): There's a well-known formalization using a "logical relation" with types interpreted as relations on terms, and the key lemma: if t : T under context, then t is related to itself at T (fundamental theorem). For identity type, the relation at ∀X.X→X applied to itself gives: for all types U1,U2 and relations R between... and for all related arguments.

Given nondeterminism, we need the interpretation to be closed under the choice rules. Let's think of a robust definition.

Let's use a semantic domain: For each type T (open, with free type variables among Delta atoms) we interpret a set/relation over terms. We need to handle bound type variables occurring in T? In the fundamental theorem, we prove by induction on has_type Delta Gamma t T. The type T may contain Ty_BVar indices? Let's see: in T_TAbs, we go from `has_type (X::Delta) Gamma (open_tm_ty t (Ty_FVar X)) (open_ty T (Ty_FVar X))` to `has_type Delta Gamma (tm_tabs t) (Ty_All T)`. So the type in the premise has bound vars opened with fresh atom X. In the conclusion, T may contain Ty_BVar 0 which is bound by the All. In the induction hypothesis (on the premise), the type is `open_ty T (Ty_FVar X)`, which contains Ty_FVar X and possibly other Ty_BVar indices referring to *other* binders (from enclosing Alls). Hmm, wait: when we have a nested type like Ty_All (Ty_All ...), the inner type after opening... Let's trace: T = Ty_All (Ty_All (Ty_BVar 0)). T_TAbs premise type = open_ty T X = Ty_All (open_ty_rec 1 X (Ty_All (Ty_BVar 0))) = Ty_All (Ty_All (open_ty_rec 2 X (Ty_BVar 0))) = Ty_All (Ty_All (Ty_BVar 0)). Hmm! That gives a type still containing Ty_BVar 0, but the inner binder's variable... There's an issue: with this "open" scheme, opening index 0 under two binders replaces index 2, not index 0. So how is `∀X.∀Y. X → Y` represented? Outermost binder opened first? Let's think: Type `Ty_All (Ty_All (Ty_Arrow (Ty_BVar 1) (Ty_BVar 0)))`? Standard de Bruijn for ∀X.∀Y.X→Y is ∀.∀.(1 → 0)? Wait standard de Bruijn: inside two binders, inner binder Y = 0, outer X = 1. But open_ty (the body under outer binder?) Hmm. T_TAbs at the outer: T = Ty_All (Ty_Arrow (Ty_BVar ?) ...)? Let's carefully define representation: `Ty_All T` where T is the body with the *newly introduced* type variable referred to by index 0 at the top level of T (since open_ty T U replaces index 0 at k=0). Under one nested binder, the outer binder's variable is index 1: because open_ty_rec increments k under binders, so opening outer binder with U replaces index 1 inside one inner binder. So representation is: outermost binder = 0 at top-level of its body, but inside k nested binders it's index k. This is exactly de Bruijn *levels* where the outermost binder gets level 0 and inner binders get increasing levels! Let's verify with ∀X.∀Y.X: body of outer ∀ is `Ty_All (Ty_Arrow (Ty_BVar 1) (Ty_BVar 0))`? Inside inner binder (depth 1 relative to outer), X should be index 1, Y index 0. open_ty_rec 0 U (Ty_All (...)): under the inner All, k=1, so replacing index 1 = X with U: yes. And Y is index 0, remains bound (until opened next). So representation: de Bruijn *levels* with outermost smallest? Hmm but then `lc_ty_at k (Ty_BVar i)` requires i < k: under k binders, valid indices are 0..k-1, and referring to the innermost binder is index k-1? Wait we just said Y (innermost) = 0. Under depth 1 (inside inner All), k=1, allowed i<1 → i=0, but we said X (outer) is index 1 there. Contradiction! Let me recheck lc_ty_at:

```
| lc_ty_bvar : forall k i, i < k -> lc_ty_at k (Ty_BVar i)
| lc_ty_all : forall k T, lc_ty_at (S k) T -> lc_ty_at k (Ty_All T)
```

For ∀.∀.(1→0): lc_ty_at 0 (All (All (Arrow (BVar 1) (BVar 0)))) requires lc_ty_at 1 (All (Arrow (BVar 1)(BVar 0))) requires lc_ty_at 2 (Arrow (BVar 1)(BVar 0)) requires lc_ty_at 2 (BVar 1) (1<2 ok) and lc_ty_at 2 (BVar 0) (0<2 ok). So both allowed. So this is consistent: index i < k where k = number of enclosing binders. Standard de Bruijn indices: innermost binder = 0. Then what does open_ty_rec do? It replaces index k where k = number of binders entered so far from the start. At top (no binders entered but we are inside the body of the binder being opened), k=0; entering an inner binder, k=1. So inside one inner binder, it replaces index 1, which in standard de Bruijn refers to the outer binder (the one being opened). Yes! Because with standard de Bruijn, at depth 1, the outer binder is index 1, and the inner binder is index 0. So open_ty_rec opens the binder that is k levels out. That is exactly correct! I earlier made an arithmetic confusion. Great.

So representation is standard de Bruijn indices, and open_ty T U opens the outermost binder of T's context (where T is the body).

Now, the fundamental theorem must handle types with de Bruijn indices. Standard approach: interpret types relative to a semantic type environment ρ : list of "relations" (or predicates), where ρ is a list indexed by de Bruijn levels? Hmm. Since types under binders have indices referring to enclosing binders, and the fundamental theorem inducts through typing rules that open binders with fresh free type variables (Ty_FVar X), the types in the induction are open with respect to *type abstraction*, but there may still be de Bruijn indices free if nested? Standard binary parametricity: Define ⟦T⟧(η1, η2, ρ) where η_i : type substitutions, ρ maps type variables to R. The relation is on terms of types η1(T) and η2(T). The clauses:
- ⟦X⟧ = ρ(X) (relation on values of η1(X), η2(X)).

Actually, the semantic universal interpretation should quantify over U1,U2 and R, but for the operational clauses in the arrow case, we need to type-check values? Do we? Let's carefully design the value/term relations.

Let's define:

```
Definition VRel := tm -> tm -> Prop.

Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> ty) (rho : atom -> VRel) (v1 v2 : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => False  (* only closed types at the top; bound vars handled by a benv *)
  ...
```

We need de Bruijn env for bound variables. Let's include it:

```
Definition benv := list (ty * ty * VRel).
Fixpoint VR (benv : benv) (T : ty) (sigma1 sigma2 : atom -> ty) (rho : atom -> VRel) (v1 v2 : tm) : Prop :=
 match T with
 | Ty_BVar i => match nth_error benv i with Some (U1,U2,R) => R v1 v2 | None => False end
 | Ty_FVar X => rho X v1 v2
 | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
     exists T1' T2', ??? 
```

Hmm, wait. If the type T is opened by substitutions sigma on free atoms, then `Ty_Arrow T1 T2` as a syntactic type corresponds to left type `Ty_Arrow (subst sigma1 T1) (subst sigma2 T2)`; we don't need to store it. The value relation at arrow between v1 : arrow type and v2 : arrow type should quantify over related arguments w1,w2 at T1 and require the applications term-related at T2.

But careful: With a de Bruijn env, the types in benv correspond to the bound variable's *types* on each side? In the semantic universal, at `Ty_All T` we quantify over U1, U2 and R. Now, which is simpler: unary or binary? With unary, the fundamental theorem's statement:

```
Lemma fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T, has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall σ ρ γ,
    (forall X, In X Delta -> wf/lc (σ X)) ->
    (forall X, ρ X is a predicate on values, maybe with wf_ty [] (σ X) or lc) ->
    (forall x T', lookup_context x Gamma = Some T' -> wf_ty Delta T' -> 
        VR_lc? and VR (T' under σ,ρ) (γ x)) ->
    TR T (σ,ρ) (apply σ γ t)
```

So the semantics:

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (benv : list VRel) (rho : atom -> VRel) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => match nth_error benv i with Some R => R v | None => False end
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X v
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => forall w, value w -> VR T1 benv rho w -> TR T2 benv rho (tm_app v w)
  | Ty_All T1 => forall U P, locally_closed_ty U -> TR T1 (P :: benv) rho (tm_tapp v U)
  end
with TR (T : ty) (benv : list VRel) (rho : atom -> VRel) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ VR T benv rho v.
```

Let me define the type substitution on types (simultaneous):

```
Fixpoint ty_subst_env (σ : atom -> ty) (T : ty) : ty :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => Ty_BVar i
  | Ty_FVar X => σ X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => Ty_Arrow (ty_subst_env σ T1) (ty_subst_env σ T2)
  | Ty_All T1 => Ty_All (ty_subst_env σ T1)
  end.
```

Since bvars are de Bruijn, the substitution under Ty_All is fine (no capture of atoms).

Term type substitution:
```
Fixpoint tm_ty_subst_env σ t :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => tm_fvar x
  | tm_abs T b => tm_abs (ty_subst_env σ T) (tm_ty_subst_env σ b)
  | tm_app t1 t2 => ...
  | tm_tabs b => tm_tabs (tm_ty_subst_env σ b)
  | tm_tapp t1 T => tm_tapp (tm_ty_subst_env σ t1) (ty_subst_env σ T)
  | tm_choice ...
  end.
```
Note this doesn't open bound type bvars (de Bruijn, correct).

Term variable substitution by environment γ:
```
Fixpoint tm_subst_env (γ : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => γ x
  | tm_abs T b => tm_abs T (tm_subst_env γ b)
  | ...
  end.
```
No capture issue because type annotations and term vars separate; term binders are de Bruijn and untouched, and γ values may contain free atoms but no de Bruijn indices? γ values should be values, hence locally closed (lc_tm_at 0 0), so substituting them under binders is safe in this de Bruijn setup? Wait: substituting a *closed* term (lc_tm_at 0 0) into a hole under de Bruijn binders: our simple substitution replaces free atoms, and the term's de Bruijn indices are unaffected. The hole here is a free atom (tm_fvar x), not a de Bruijn position. Term variables are atoms; there's no shifting needed. The substituted term γ x is lc (no free bvars) so it won't capture or be captured. Good.

Then `apply σ γ t := tm_ty_subst_env σ (tm_subst_env γ t)`. This substitutes term vars then type atoms; note type substitution also affects the types inside γ(x). Good.

Do we need γ values to be lc for substitution lemmas? Not necessarily for equalities, but for the semantic env hypothesis (values), yes.

Commutation lemma: `tm_ty_subst_env σ (open_tm t u) = open_tm (tm_ty_subst_env σ t) (tm_ty_subst_env σ u)`? Let's check: open_tm_rec k u t replaces de Bruijn k with u; type subst commutes since it doesn't touch bvars. Yes, provable by induction. More generally for open_tm_rec k.

And `tm_subst_env γ (open_tm t (tm_fvar x)) = open_tm (tm_subst_env γ t) (γ x)` when x ∉ FV_tm(t) (free term atoms). This is the key lemma; requires a free-variable function and freshness. Let's define FV for terms (atoms) and FV_ty for type atoms.

Actually, we can avoid proving the equality with freshness by using a different tactic: Since the T_Abs rule quantifies over all x ∉ L, and L is a finite list we can extend? We cannot change L. But we can choose x fresh from L ∪ FV(t2) ∪ dom Gamma. Need FV(t2) defined. Fine.

Similarly for T_TAbs choose X fresh from L ∪ FV_ty(t) ∪ FV_ty of... The substitution composition: tm_ty_subst_env σ' (open_tm_ty t X) = open_tm_ty (tm_ty_subst_env σ t) (σ' X) when X ∉ FV_ty(t)? Here σ' = σ[X↦U]. In the T_TAbs case, the opened term is open_tm_ty t (Ty_FVar X); applying σ' (which maps X to U): since X is fresh and appears only at opened positions, result = open_tm_ty (tm_ty_subst_env σ t) U. Need X not free in t's type annotations and type args, i.e., X ∉ FV_ty(t). Good.

Now, the fundamental theorem's statement. Let's write it in the unary style:

```
Lemma fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall (σ : atom -> ty) (ρ : atom -> VRel) (γ : atom -> tm),
    (forall X, In X Delta -> locally_closed_ty (σ X)) ->
    (forall x T', lookup_context x Gamma = Some T' ->
        VR T' [] ρ (γ x)) ->   (* maybe plus value? VR implies value? *)
    TR T [] ρ (apply σ γ t).
```

Hmm, issues:
1. The term env hypothesis: VR T' [] ρ (γ x) — but VR at T' with what σ? The type T' is wf over Delta; the actual type of γ(x) should be σ(T'), but VR doesn't use σ. We need γ(x) to be a value; define VR to have `value` built in? Let's define VR to take only values and add a separate `value (γ x)` hypothesis. Or define VR T ρ v to imply value v. For Ty_FVar, ρ X v arbitrary; we can require the environment ρ to be a predicate on values only, but the hypothesis should include value. Let's include in the term env hypothesis: `locally_closed_tm (γ x) /\ VR T' [] ρ (γ x)`. Actually if VR always implies value, lc follows. We can define VR with a value requirement at every clause? E.g.:

```
VR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v := value v /\ forall w, value w -> VR T1 w -> TR T2 (tm_app v w)
```
For Ty_All: `value v /\ forall U P, lc_ty U -> TR T1 [P] (tm_tapp v U)`.
For Ty_FVar: `value v /\ ρ X v`.
For Ty_BVar: `value v /\ benv...`.
For TR: `exists v, t -->* v /\ VR T v` (VR includes value).
Then VR implies value. But requiring value at every type is harmless: our semantic universal quantifies P over predicates; the type variable predicate ρ X is applied to values only but we can define VR at fvar as value v /\ ρ X v. The final instantiation P = fun w => w = v; we need VR at X for v: value v /\ v=v ✓.

Hmm, but for the T_TAbs universal clause, VR (Ty_All T) v requires `value v` — we get it from the value being tm_tabs (from TR). Fine.

2. The type argument U in the universal clause must be locally closed. But the term's type application step requires `locally_closed_ty U`. In the universal clause, we quantify over lc U. In the T_TApp case, the type U from the judgment: we need lc_ty U where U is the syntax in the judgment; but under σ, the actual type argument in the term is σ(U), and the step requires lc_ty (σ U). We need σ maps atoms in Delta to lc types and wf→lc. Also the universal clause's TR at binder T with P = ? The T_TApp case: IH gives TR (Ty_All T) [] ρ (apply σ γ t). Unfold TR: exists v, apply... t -->* v, VR (Ty_All T) v. Unfold VR: forall U' P lcU', TR T [P] (tm_tapp v U'). We need TR (open_ty T U) [] ρ (tm_tapp (apply σ γ t) (σ U)). Hmm, the term relation at type open_ty T U with empty benv. The universal gives TR T [P] (tm_tapp v U') for chosen U',P. To match, we need the opening lemma in the other direction: TR (open_ty T U) [] ρ ≃ TR T [P] with P = VR of U? Wait, now the term relation at open_ty T U (a type with U substituted) should equal the binder interpretation with predicate = the semantic predicate of U, i.e., VR U [] ρ. So choose P := VR U [] ρ (as a predicate on values) and U' := σ U. Then the opening lemma: TR (open_ty T U) [] ρ ≃ TR T [P] (with P = VR U [] ρ). Is that right? The opening lemma earlier was for opening with a *fresh atom* Y: TR (open_ty T Y) [] ρ[Y↦P] ≃ TR T [P]. Now opening with U: TR (open_ty T U) [] ρ ≃ TR T [P] where P should interpret U's free atoms via ρ and U's structure. So P := fun v => VR U [] ρ v. And this is exactly the "substitution lemma": interpreting T with a binder predicate equal to the semantics of U gives the semantics of open_ty T U.

So we need TWO substitution lemmas:
(a) Opening with a fresh atom: `TR (open_ty T Y) [] ρ[Y↦P] <-> TR T [P] ρ`, used in T_TAbs.
(b) Opening with an arbitrary type U: `TR (open_ty T U) [] ρ <-> TR T [VR U [] ρ] ρ`, used in T_TApp.
And (b) is more general. Actually (b) with U = Ty_FVar Y gives (a) if VR (Ty_FVar Y) [] ρ = fun v => value v /\ ρ Y v, which is not exactly P (it adds value). Hmm, slight mismatch. We can define the benv entries as predicates on values and handle `value` separately; we need to be careful with the value conjuncts. Let's design VR to make these lemmas clean.

Alternative: define VR without `value` conjuncts for variables (assume predicates on values) and have a separate `is_value` requirement in TR/V. Let's think about the cleanest definitions.

Standard logical predicate definition:
```
VR T ρ v :=  (* v is a value; we assume value v as a hypothesis everywhere *)
  match T with
  | BVar i => benv i v
  | FVar X => ρ X v
  | Arrow T1 T2 => forall w, value w -> VR T1 ρ w -> TR T2 ρ (app v w)
  | All T1 => forall U P, lc U -> TR T1 (P::ρ) (tapp v U)
  end
TR T ρ t := exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ VR T ρ v.
```
With this, VR doesn't imply value. In the arrow clause, the argument w has value hypothesised. In the universal clause, tapp v U: for this to reduce, v must be a tm_tabs; that's ensured by VR (All) only if v is that shape; the clause is a proposition that will be false for non-tabs (for large enough U? If U lc exists — yes atom types are lc — tapp of a non-tabs doesn't reduce, so TR false). Good.

For the environment hypothesis, we need γ x to be a value and VR: include `value (γ x) /\ VR T' [] ρ (γ x)`.

Rather than working with has_type_lc and substitution preservation separately, we can prove a direct lemma: for well-typed terms, `apply σ γ t` is lc at 0 0 under the hypotheses. We'll need `has_type_lc` and substitution-lc preservation anyway.

Wait, actually for the value in TR at arrow, we need `value (tm_abs (σT1) (apply σ γ t2))`, which requires locally_closed_tm. We can get it from lc of the substituted abstraction. Let's plan lemma:
`apply_lc : forall Delta Gamma t T, has_type Delta Gamma t T -> forall σ γ, (forall X, In X Delta -> lc_ty_at 0 (σ X)) -> (forall x, lc_tm_at 0 0 (γ x)) -> lc_tm_at 0 0 (apply σ γ t)`.
Proof by induction on has_type? That's another induction similar to has_type_lc, but with substitutions. Hmm, maybe we can derive it from has_type_lc + general substitution preservation: 
`lc_tm_at 0 0 t` (from has_type_lc) and γ x lc at 0 0 and σ X lc at 0 0 → lc of apply. Actually, we might not even need has_type_lc separately if the fundamental theorem's proof can provide the value conditions through the induction. But has_type_lc is needed for terms that are not abstractions: e.g., to compose multi-steps, we needed lc of the argument t2 for ST_App1. Where would that come from? In T_App, the IH gives TR T1 (apply σ γ t2) which includes an existence of a path but not lc of the term. We need lc of the argument to apply ST_App1. Alternatively, choose x fresh from a list L' that includes the free atoms of t2. We need a function. Let's define:
```
Fixpoint fv_ty (T : ty) : list atom :=
  match T with Ty_BVar _ => [] | Ty_FVar X => [X] | Arrow T1 T2 => fv_ty T1 ++ fv_ty T2 | All T1 => fv_ty T1 end.
Fixpoint fv_tm (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with tm_bvar _ => [] | tm_fvar x => [x] | tm_abs T b => fv_ty T ++ fv_tm b | tm_app a b => fv_tm a ++ fv_tm b | tm_tabs b => fv_tm b | tm_tapp b T => fv_tm b ++ fv_ty T | tm_choice a b => fv_tm a ++ fv_tm b end.
```
And a freshness function: `fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right max 0 l)` or use `max_list`. Prove `~ In (fresh l) l`. Since atom=nat and lists finite. Implementing with fold_right max: `Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 l)`. Lemma `fresh_not_in`: forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l. Proof: forall x, In x l -> x ≤ fold_right max 0 l < fresh l. Induction on l. Straightforward with lia.

```coq
(* ---------- generic list/freshness helpers ---------- *)
Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 l).

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l. unfold fresh. intro H.
  assert (Hle : forall x, In x l -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 l).
  { induction l as [| a l IH]; simpl; intros x Hx; [contradiction|].
    destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    - apply Nat.le_max_l.
    - specialize (IH x Hx). apply Nat.le_max_r... }
```

Wait, fold_right Nat.max 0 l with simpl: fold_right max 0 (a::l) = max a (fold_right max 0 l). For x ∈ a::l: if x=a, x ≤ max a rest ✓; else IH: x ≤ rest ≤ max a rest ✓. We can use `Nat.le_max_l` and `Nat.le_max_r`? `Nat.le_max_r : forall n m, m <= Nat.max n m`? Let me check stdlib names: `Nat.le_max_l : forall n m : nat, n <= Nat.max n m`, `Nat.le_max_r : forall n m, m <= Nat.max n m`. So x ≤ fold_right max 0 l = M; max a M ≥ M ≥ x ✓.

Then `In (S M) l` implies S M ≤ M by Hle, contradict lia.

Also might need `fresh_not_in_app` etc. Actually we'll construct fresh from L ++ fv; prove ~In via fresh_not_in and In_app.

```coq
Fixpoint ty_subst_env (σ : atom -> ty) (T : ty) : ty :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => Ty_BVar i
  | Ty_FVar X => σ X
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => Ty_Arrow (ty_subst_env σ T1) (ty_subst_env σ T2)
  | Ty_All T1 => Ty_All (ty_subst_env σ T1)
  end.

Fixpoint tm_ty_subst_env (σ : atom -> ty) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => tm_fvar x
  | tm_abs T b => tm_abs (ty_subst_env σ T) (tm_ty_subst_env σ b)
  | tm_app a b => tm_app (tm_ty_subst_env σ a) (tm_ty_subst_env σ b)
  | tm_tabs b => tm_tabs (tm_ty_subst_env σ b)
  | tm_tapp b T => tm_tapp (tm_ty_subst_env σ b) (ty_subst_env σ T)
  | tm_choice a b => tm_choice (tm_ty_subst_env σ a) (tm_ty_subst_env σ b)
  end.

Fixpoint tm_subst_env (γ : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => γ x
  | tm_abs T b => tm_abs T (tm_subst_env γ b)
  | tm_app a b => tm_app (tm_subst_env γ a) (tm_subst_env γ b)
  | tm_tabs b => tm_tabs (tm_subst_env γ b)
  | tm_tapp b T => tm_tapp (tm_subst_env γ b) T
  | tm_choice a b => tm_choice (tm_subst_env γ a) (tm_subst_env γ b)
  end.

Definition apply_env (σ : atom -> ty) (γ : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  tm_subst_env γ (tm_ty_subst_env σ t).
```

FV functions as above.

Now, the semantic section.

Definitions:
```coq
Definition VRel := tm -> tm -> Prop.  (* unary actually tm -> Prop; let's call PRel *)

Definition PRel := tm -> Prop.
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => match nth_error benv i with Some P => P v | None => False end
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X v
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      forall w, value w -> VR T1 benv rho w -> TR T2 benv rho (tm_app v w)
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      forall U P, locally_closed_ty U -> TR T1 (P :: benv) rho (tm_tapp v U)
  end
with TR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed_tm t /\ exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ VR T benv rho v.
```
Coq supports mutual Fixpoints with a Prop return? Should be fine (strict positivity? VR and TR are Fixpoints returning Prop; mutual recursion). The recursion: VR calls TR on the same T (arrow case: TR T2 is a subterm; All case: TR T1 subterm) and TR calls VR T. Coq's guard checker should accept: each recursive call is on a subterm (T1/T2). The types: VR : ty -> list PRel -> (atom -> PRel) -> tm -> Prop; TR same with t. Mutual fixpoint accepted if calls are structurally decreasing on T. In VR Arrow: TR T2 ... ; in VR All: TR T1 ...; TR: VR T ... . All subterms of the matched T. Should pass.

Note: `PRel := tm -> Prop` is a function type, not an inductive; putting it in a list is fine.

Then lemmas:
- `TR_mono_step` etc.
- `VR_benv_agree`.
- `VR_rho_agree`.
- `TR_benv_agree` and `TR_rho_agree`: since TR = lc ∧ exists v, ... VR at same T; pointwise equivalence of VR for all v gives TR equivalence. The VR equivalence statements depend on T and same term v; for TR we can derive with a lemma:
```
Lemma TR_benv_agree : forall k T, lc_ty_at k T -> forall benv1 benv2 rho t,
  benv_agree k benv1 benv2 -> (TR T benv1 rho t <-> TR T benv2 rho t).
```
But this is essentially VR_benv_agree at the VR level plus unfolding TR; we can prove TR_benv_agree by unfolding TR and using VR_benv_agree, but that requires VR_benv_agree already proven; VR_benv_agree's All case needs TR_benv_agree for bodies? Let's check: VR_benv_agree for T = Ty_All T1: goal VR (All T1) benv1 ↔ VR (All T1) benv2 = forall U P lcU, TR T1 (P::benv1) ↔ TR T1 (P::benv2). So it needs TR_benv_agree at T1 with benv (P::benv1) vs (P::benv2), which needs VR_benv_agree at T1 with agreement level... The induction: if we prove VR_benv_agree by induction on lc_ty_at k T, the All case lc_ty_at (S k) T1; we need TR_benv_agree at T1 with k' = S k and benv P::...; but TR_benv_agree in turn unfolds to VR_benv_agree at T1 with level S k, which is exactly the IH (if we prove them mutually). So prove VR_benv_agree and TR_benv_agree mutually by induction on the lc_ty_at derivation, or simply prove a single lemma about VR and derive TR: 
```
Lemma TR_benv_agree_from_VR : (forall v, VR T benv1 rho v <-> VR T benv2 rho v) -> TR T benv1 rho t <-> TR T benv2 rho t.
```
and in VR_benv_agree's All case, use it with the IH at T1 (which is exactly the forall-v equivalence for T1? The IH from induction on lc_ty_at (S k) T1 gives VR_benv_agree at T1 for level S k with arbitrary benv1 benv2 agreeing at S k). Yes: the induction hypothesis for the subderivation lc_ty_at (S k) T1 is `forall benv1 benv2 rho v, benv_agree (S k) benv1 benv2 -> VR T1 benv1 rho v ↔ VR T1 benv2 rho v`. Then to prove TR T1 (P::benv1) ↔ TR T1 (P::benv2), use TR_benv_agree_from_VR with the IH applied to P::benv1, P::benv2 (agreement S k holds as shown) — for any v. So we don't need mutual induction; just a helper that lifts a pointwise VR equivalence to TR. Good.

Similarly VR_rho_agree by induction on lc_ty_at k T? The rho agreement needed: rho1 and rho2 agree on free atoms of T. But when under an All, the body T1's free atoms: T1 may contain the All's bound bvar; free *atoms* are the same as T's. So the induction can be on a condition `forall X, In X (fv_ty T) -> forall v, rho1 X v <-> rho2 X v`. But the All case: benv extended; VR T1 benv rho1 ↔ VR T1 benv rho2, needs the same condition for T1, which follows from fv_ty (All T1) = fv_ty T1 (by definition, fv_ty All is fv_ty body). Great, no need for lc. So:
```
Lemma VR_rho_agree : forall T benv rho1 rho2 v,
  (forall X, In X (fv_ty T) -> forall v, rho1 X v <-> rho2 X v) ->
  (VR T benv rho1 v <-> VR T benv rho2 v).
```
Proof by induction on T (structural!) — but the All case calls VR on T1 (subterm) ✓; Arrow subterms ✓; BVar/FVar ✓. TR version via pointwise lifting? To prove TR_rho_agree we can similarly induct on T, or use the helper `TR_from_VR_equiv` with the VR equivalence for all v. Since VR_rho_agree gives forall v equivalence at T, lifting gives TR. Good.

Now the type substitution lemma (VR_subst):
```
Lemma VR_open_ty_rec_subst : forall j T benv rho U,
  length benv = j ->
  locally_closed_ty U ->
  lc_ty_at (S j) T ->
  forall v, VR (open_ty_rec j U T) benv rho v <->
            VR T (benv ++ [VR U benv rho]) rho v.
```
Wait, RHS uses `VR U benv rho` as a PRel. But U's interpretation should perhaps be under the *current* benv including internal binders? Since U lc, VR U benv rho is independent of benv (by VR_benv_agree with k=0). Let's use R := VR U benv rho; in the All case, the inner RHS benv becomes P::(benv++[R]) and LHS benv becomes P::benv; the lemma needs R' := VR U (P::benv) rho to equal R; by VR_benv_agree with lc U (k=0), pointwise equivalent. Then TR equivalence can use the pointwise VR equivalence to swap. Hmm, but the lemma's RHS uses benv ++ [R]; in the All case, the needed instantiation of the IH is at T1 with j'=S j and benv' = P::benv, and RHS' = (P::benv) ++ [VR U (P::benv) rho]. We want to compare with P :: (benv ++ [R]) = (P::benv) ++ [R]. Since VR U (P::benv) rho ↔ VR U benv rho pointwise (VR_benv_agree), the TR statements are equivalent by the TR pointwise lifting. But the RHS of our goal is `VR T1 (P :: (benv ++ [R])) rho v'`, and the IH gives `VR T1 ((P::benv) ++ [VR U (P::benv) rho]) rho v'`. These benvs differ only at the last entry, where the entries are pointwise-equivalent predicates (R' vs R). So we need a lemma: if two benvs are pointwise equivalent as predicates (for all i, nth_error gives pointwise-equivalent PRels), then VR T benv1 rho v ↔ VR T benv2 rho v. This generalizes benv_agree. We can define:

```
Definition benv_pointwise (benv1 benv2 : list PRel) : Prop :=
  forall i P1 P2, nth_error benv1 i = Some P1 -> nth_error benv2 i = Some P2 -> forall v, P1 v <-> P2 v.
```
Then prove `VR_benv_pequiv` by induction on T: BVar: use the pointwise; All: extend with the same P (pointwise); Arrow ✓. And TR version. This subsumes benv_agree? benv_agree gives equal entries, hence pointwise. But for lc independence we need only first k entries; we can express with a stronger notion: benv_pointwise_up_to k. Let's define:

```
Definition benv_agree (k : nat) (b1 b2 : list PRel) : Prop :=
  forall i P1 P2, i < k -> nth_error b1 i = Some P1 -> nth_error b2 i = Some P2 -> forall v, P1 v <-> P2 v.
```
Then VR_benv_agree: lc_ty_at k T -> benv_agree k b1 b2 -> VR T b1 rho v ↔ VR T b2 rho v. Proof induction on lc_ty_at k T:
- bvar i: i<k; both nth_error; use agreement. If one is None, VR is False on that side; agreement only handles Some/Some. Hmm, if b1 has Some P1 and b2 has None, then RHS VR false; but can we conclude? With lc_ty_at k T ensuring i<k, but the lists might be of different lengths. benv_agree as defined doesn't prevent one from being None. Let's define agreement to require both Some with pointwise equivalence, or both None:
```
Definition benv_agree k b1 b2 :=
  forall i, i < k -> (forall v, (match nth_error b1 i with Some P => P v | None => False end)
                              <-> (match nth_error b2 i with Some P => P v | None => False end)).
```
Simpler: define `lookup_rel b i := match nth_error b i with Some P => P | None => fun _ => False end`, and agreement `forall i, i<k -> forall v, lookup_rel b1 i v <-> lookup_rel b2 i v`. Then bvar case trivial. All case: extend with P: need agreement at S k between P::b1 and P::b2: for i<S k, i=0 both P; i=S i' with i'<k, use agreement. ✓. This is clean.

Similarly define "pointwise equivalence of benvs" (all i) and prove VR_benv_pequiv with induction on T (not on lc), used for the substitution lemma's last-entry swap. But maybe VR_benv_agree with k = length? Not exactly. Let's define `benv_pequiv b1 b2 := forall i v, lookup_rel b1 i v <-> lookup_rel b2 b2 i v`. Then VR_benv_pequiv : forall T, benv_pequiv b1 b2 -> forall v, VR T b1 rho v <-> VR T b2 rho v. Proof by induction on T. All case: extend with same P; pointwise holds. ✓. And TR_benv_pequiv via lifting.

Then the substitution lemma's induction:
- BVar: as before.
- FVar ✓.
- Arrow: IH both.
- All T1: as discussed: use IH at T1 with j'=S j, benv'=P::benv, but the RHS predicate should be VR U (P::benv) rho. Good.

We have IH1: VR T1 b1 ↔ VR T1 b2 (from lc_ty_at k T1); and need TR T2 b1 (app v w) ↔ TR T2 b2 (app v w) (from lc_ty_at k T2). TR equivalence follows from VR equivalence for all v (lifting lemma) at T2, using IH2. So we need the lifting lemma `TR_congr` which itself is straightforward given pointwise VR equivalence. It can be proven directly by unfolding TR (no induction). Good.
- All: goal forall U P, TR T1 (P::b1) ↔ TR T1 (P::b2); use lifting + IH at T1 with benv agreement S k. ✓.
So `VR_benv_agree` by induction on lc_ty_at k T (with k general), and `TR_benv_agree` derived via lifting from the pointwise VR equivalence (forall v, from VR_benv_agree at T with k). Good.

`VR_rho_agree`: induction on T structurally; Arrow case needs TR equivalence at T2 via lifting with IH at T2; All case needs TR at T1 with benv P::benv and rho1/rho2; the free atom condition for T1 follows from fv_ty (All T1) = fv_ty T1; need the condition as a hypothesis for T1; we can prove via a helper: if condition holds for T, it holds for subterms with fv_ty sub ⊆ fv_ty T. We'll structure the induction on T with the hypothesis `rho_agree_on (fv_ty T) rho1 rho2` and in the All case derive for T1: fv_ty T = fv_ty T1 (definitionally by the fixpoint: fv_ty (All T1) = fv_ty T1; simpl may reduce). Similarly Arrow: fv_ty (Arrow T1 T2) = fv_ty T1 ++ fv_ty T2; need agreement on each part; derive from In_app. We'll write helper lemmas or just intros X HX; apply H; apply in_or_app; auto.

Alternatively, define rho agreement by a predicate `agree_rho rho1 rho2 T := forall X v, In X (fv_ty T) -> (rho1 X v <-> rho2 X v)`. In the induction, for subterms we need to derive agreement from the parent's. We can prove generic lemmas: `fv_ty T1 ⊆ fv_ty (Arrow T1 T2)` (simpl: In_app_l), `fv_ty T1 ⊆ fv_ty (All T1)` (equal). Use `intros X HX; apply Hagree; apply in_or_app; left; exact HX`, and for All: `simpl in *` or `change (In X (fv_ty T1))`. Since fv_ty (Ty_All T1) reduces to fv_ty T1 by simpl, fine.

Now the type substitution lemma's proof needs the benv_pequiv lifting. Let's define:
```
Definition lookup_rel (b : list PRel) (i : nat) : PRel :=
  match nth_error b i with Some P => P | None => fun _ => False end.
Definition benv_agree (k : nat) (b1 b2 : list PRel) : Prop :=
  forall i, i < k -> forall v, lookup_rel b1 i v <-> lookup_rel b2 i v.
Definition benv_pequiv (b1 b2 : list PRel) : Prop :=
  forall i v, lookup_rel b1 i v <-> lookup_rel b2 i v.
```
Lemma benv_agree_cons: benv_agree k b1 b2 -> benv_agree (S k) (P::b1) (P::b2).
Lemma benv_pequiv_cons: benv_pequiv b1 b2 -> benv_pequiv (P::b1) (P::b2).
Lemma benv_pequiv_append_last: benv_pequiv b1 b2 -> (forall v, P1 v <-> P2 v) -> benv_pequiv (b1++[P1]) (b2++[P2])? Prove `benv_pequiv_append : benv_pequiv b1 b2 -> (forall v, P1 v <-> P2 v) -> benv_pequiv (b1++[P1]) (b2++[P2])`. By induction on b1/b2? Since prefixes are identical, easier: prove lookup_rel (l++[P]) i = if i = length l then P else lookup_rel l i, then benv_pequiv follows from b1=b2. Actually in our use the prefixes are literally identical, so benv_pequiv (b++[R']) (b++[R]) iff R'~R. Prove a direct lemma:
```
Lemma lookup_rel_app_single : forall l P i, lookup_rel (l ++ [P]) i =

```

Lemma benv_pequiv_append_single : forall l P1 P2,
  (forall v, P1 v <-> P2 v) -> benv_pequiv (l++[P1]) (l++[P2]).

Lemma multi_trans : forall a b c, multi a b -> multi b c -> multi a c.

  intros a b c H1 H2. revert c H2. induction H1; intros c H2.

```coq
(* ==================== Auxiliary definitions ==================== *)

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 l).

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l H.
  unfold fresh in H.
  assert (Hle : forall x, In x l -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 l).
  { induction l as [| a l IH]; simpl; intros x Hx.
    - contradiction.
    - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
      + apply Nat.le_max_l.
      + apply Nat.le_trans with (fold_right Nat.max 0 l); [apply IH; exact Hx | apply Nat.le_max_r]. }
  pose proof (Hle _ H). lia.
Qed.
```

Wait, `fold_right Nat.max 0 (a::l)` simplifies to `Nat.max a (fold_right Nat.max 0 l)`? fold_right f a [x1;x2] = f x1 (f x2 a). So fold_right max 0 (a::l) = max a (fold_right max 0 l) ✓. Nat.le_max_l : forall n m, n <= Nat.max n m; Nat.le_max_r : forall n m, m <= Nat.max n m. Good.

FV:
```coq
Fixpoint fv_ty (T : ty) : list atom :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar _ => []
  | Ty_FVar X => [X]
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fv_ty T1 ++ fv_ty T2
  | Ty_All T1 => fv_ty T1
  end.

Fixpoint fv_tm (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_abs T b => fv_ty T ++ fv_tm b
  | tm_app a b => fv_tm a ++ fv_tm b
  | tm_tabs b => fv_tm b
  | tm_tapp b T => fv_tm b ++ fv_ty T
  | tm_choice a b => fv_tm a ++ fv_tm b
  end.

Fixpoint fv_context (Gamma : context) : list atom :=
  match Gamma with
  | [] => []
  | (_, T) :: Gamma' => fv_ty T ++ fv_context Gamma'
  end.
```

Fixpoint tm_ty_subst_env ...
Fixpoint tm_subst_env ...
Definition apply_env ...
```

Correct lemmas:

```coq
Lemma ty_subst_env_agree_fv : forall sigma1 sigma2 T,
  (forall X, In X (fv_ty T) -> sigma1 X = sigma2 X) ->
  ty_subst_env sigma1 T = ty_subst_env sigma2 T.
Proof.
  intros sigma1 sigma2 T. induction T; simpl; intros Hagree.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply Hagree. simpl. auto.
  - f_equal.
    + apply IHT1. intros X HX. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact HX.
    + apply IHT2. intros X HX. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. exact HX.
  - f_equal. apply IHT. intros X HX. apply Hagree. simpl. exact HX.
Qed.

Lemma fv_tm_ty_irrel : forall t sigma1 sigma2,
  (forall X, In X (fv_tm t) -> sigma1 X = sigma2 X) ->
  tm_ty_subst_env sigma1 t = tm_ty_subst_env sigma2 t.
Proof.
  intros t. induction t; simpl; intros sigma1 sigma2 Hagree.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - f_equal.
    + apply ty_subst_env_agree_fv. intros X HX. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact HX.
    + apply IHt. intros X HX. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. exact HX.
  - f_equal; [apply IHt1 | apply IHt2]; intros X HX; apply Hagree; simpl; apply in_or_app;
      [left | right]; exact HX.
  - apply IHt. intros X HX. apply Hagree. simpl. exact HX.
  - f_equal.
    + apply IHt. intros X HX. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact HX.
    + apply ty_subst_env_agree_fv. intros X HX. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. exact HX.
  - f_equal; [apply IHt1 | apply IHt2]; intros X HX; apply Hagree; simpl; apply in_or_app;
      [left | right]; exact HX.
Qed.
```

Lemma tm_subst_env_agree : forall gamma1 gamma2 t,
  (forall x, In x (fv_tm t) -> gamma1 x = gamma2 x) ->
  tm_subst_env gamma1 t = tm_subst_env gamma2 t.
Proof.
  intros gamma1 gamma2 t. induction t; simpl; intros Hagree.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply Hagree. simpl. auto.
  - f_equal. apply IHt. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hx.
  - f_equal; [apply IHt1 | apply IHt2]; intros x Hx; apply Hagree; simpl; apply in_or_app;
      [left | right]; exact Hx.
  - apply IHt. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. exact Hx.
  - apply IHt. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hx.
  - f_equal; [apply IHt1 | apply IHt2]; intros x Hx; apply Hagree; simpl; apply in_or_app;
      [left | right]; exact Hx.
Qed.
```

Lemma ty_subst_env_open_ty_rec : forall sigma k U T,
  ty_subst_env sigma (open_ty_rec k U T) =
  open_ty_rec k (ty_subst_env sigma U) (ty_subst_env sigma T).
Proof.
  intros sigma k U T. revert k. induction T; intros k; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n); reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - f_equal; auto.
  - f_equal. apply IHT. 
Qed.
```

```
Proof.
  intros sigma k U T. revert k U. induction T; intros k U; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n); reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - f_equal; [apply IHT1 | apply IHT2].
  - f_equal. apply IHT.

Monotonicity lemmas:
```
Lemma lc_ty_mono : forall k k' T, lc_ty_at k T -> k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T.
Proof. intros k k' T H. induction H; intros Hle; eauto using lc_ty_at.
  - constructor. apply IHlc_ty_all. lia.
Qed.
```
Careful with constructor names generated by Coq: lc_ty_bvar, lc_ty_fvar, lc_ty_arrow, lc_ty_all. In induction H, the cases are named. For lc_ty_bvar k i Hi: need lc_ty_at k' (bvar i): i < k' from i<k≤k' lia. `constructor; lia`. For arrow: IH... Let's write directly:
```
Lemma lc_ty_mono : forall k k' T, lc_ty_at k T -> k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T.
Proof.
  intros k k' T H. induction H; intros Hle.
  - apply lc_ty_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_ty_fvar.
  - apply lc_ty_arrow; [apply IHlc_ty_at1 | apply IHlc_ty_at2]; exact Hle.
  - apply lc_ty_all. apply IHlc_ty_at. lia.
Qed.
```
Hmm, the induction hypotheses in the arrow case are `forall k', k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T1`? Since the goal has k' universally? I wrote `intros k k' T H. induction H; intros Hle`. The `k'` was introduced before induction, so IH would be about the same k' and subterms: IHlc_ty_at1 : k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T1. Lemma lc_ty_mono : forall k k' T, lc_ty_at k T -> k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T.

But the statement has k as a parameter of the inductive (lc_ty_at k T); `induction H` after `intros k T H` will have the IH for subderivations at possibly different k (for lc_ty_all, the subderivation is lc_ty_at (S k) T1). The motives need to generalize T and k. Let's do `induction H` and see. Actually `intros k T H. induction H` yields cases where IH are `forall k', ...`? Since k is fixed but the all case has a different parameter S k, Coq's induction on the derivation will allow IH with the subderivation's parameters if the goal is generalized over k? If k is fixed in the context, the motive is `fun (k:nat) (T:ty) (H:lc_ty_at k T) => forall k', k<=k' -> lc_ty_at k' T`, so yes we need to generalize. Coq automatically generalizes the indices when doing induction on H if we use the right form: `induction H` where the goal mentions k, T. It will produce motives over the indices. Usually `induction H` with `lc_ty_at` works if the goal is about the indices. To be safe:
```
Lemma lc_ty_mono : forall k k' T, lc_ty_at k T -> k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T.
Proof.
  intros k k' T H. revert k'. induction H; intros k' Hle.
```
Here H's indices k,T are fixed before revert? We intros k k' T H; revert k'. Then induction H: the goal `forall k', lc_ty_at k' T` with indices k,T abstracted by induction; IH generated as `forall k', lc_ty_at k' T1`? For the all case, subderivation lc_ty_at (S k) T1; the motive `fun k T H => forall k', lc_ty_at k' T` does not depend on k, so IH for subderivation: forall k', lc_ty_at k' T1. Then to prove lc_ty_at k' (All T1) need lc_ty_at (S k') T1; apply IH (with k'=S k') ✓. But we also need the hypothesis k<=k'? If the motive is just `forall k', lc_ty_at k' T`, we lose monotonicity: the theorem's conclusion needs k≤k'. Better motive: `fun k T H => forall k', k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T`. With induction H, the IH for subderivation lc_ty_at (S k) T1: forall k', (S k) <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T1. In the all case, goal: k ≤ k' -> lc_ty_at k' (All T1), i.e., lc_ty_at (S k') T1. Apply IH with k' := S k'; need S k ≤ S k' from k ≤ k' ✓.
So:
```
Proof.
  intros k T H. induction H; intros k' Hle.
  - apply lc_ty_bvar; lia.
  - apply lc_ty_fvar.
  - apply lc_ty_arrow; [apply IHlc_ty_at1 | apply IHlc_ty_at2]; exact Hle.
  - apply lc_ty_all. apply IHlc_ty_at. lia.
Qed.
```
But wait, we did intros k T H without reverting k? k is an index; `induction H` with k fixed might not generalize. Let's write `intros k T H. induction H; intros k' Hle` — Coq's induction on an inductive with indices in the goal will abstract them automatically? The goal after intros is `k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T` with k fixed as a variable, not generalized. The induction principle for lc_ty_at when the goal doesn't mention the indices in a generalizable way... Coq's `induction H` typically reverts the goal's dependence on indices. To be safe, use `remember` or `revert T H` patterns. Simpler: prove monotonicity by induction on T (structural):
```
Lemma lc_ty_mono : forall T k k', lc_ty_at k T -> k <= k' -> lc_ty_at k' T.
Proof.
  induction T; intros k k' H Hle; inversion H; subst.
  - apply lc_ty_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_ty_fvar.
  - apply lc_ty_arrow; [apply IHT1 | apply IHT2]; auto.
  - apply lc_ty_all. apply IHT. exact H4. lia.
Qed.
```
Inversion names may vary; use `inversion H; subst; eauto using lc_ty_at, lc_ty_bvar`. Let's do:
```
Proof.
  induction T; intros k k' H Hle; inversion H; subst;
    try (econstructor; eauto; fail); try apply lc_ty_fvar.
  - apply lc_ty_bvar; lia.
  - apply lc_ty_all. apply IHT. assumption. lia.
Qed.
```
The Ty_All case: H : lc_ty_at k (Ty_All T); inversion gives H1 : lc_ty_at (S k) T (name maybe H1). IHT : forall k k', lc_ty_at k T -> k<=k' -> lc_ty_at k' T. Apply IHT with (S k) (S k') H1; lia. econstructor may work: `apply lc_ty_all; apply IHT; [assumption|lia]`. For Arrow: inversion gives H1 : lc_ty_at k T1, H2 : lc_ty_at k T2; `apply lc_ty_arrow; [apply IHT1; [assumption|exact Hle] | apply IHT2; ...]`. Structured proof better than tactic guess.

Similarly lc_tm_mono by induction on t:
```
Lemma lc_tm_mono : forall t K k K' k', lc_tm_at K k t -> K <= K' -> k <= k' -> lc_tm_at K' k' t.
Proof.
  induction t; intros K k K' k' H HK Hk; inversion H; subst; try solve [constructor; auto; fail].
  - (* bvar *) apply lc_tm_bvar; lia.
  - (* fvar *) constructor.
  - (* abs *) apply lc_tm_abs.
    + apply lc_ty_mono with (k:=K); assumption.
    + apply IHt with (K:=K) (k:=S k); [assumption|exact HK|lia].
  - (* app *) ...
```
Names: constructors lc_tm_bvar, lc_tm_fvar, lc_tm_abs, lc_tm_app, lc_tm_tabs, lc_tm_tapp, lc_tm_choice. We'll write explicit cases with inversion; might need to name hypotheses. Let's write with `inversion H as [...]`? Simpler use `inversion H; subst; clear H` and use `assumption`/`auto`.

Let me write these tactics robustly:
```
Lemma lc_tm_mono : forall t K k K' k',
  lc_tm_at K k t -> K <= K' -> k <= k' -> lc_tm_at K' k' t.
Proof.
  induction t; intros K k K' k' H HK Hk; inversion H; subst.
  - apply lc_tm_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_tm_fvar.
  - apply lc_tm_abs.
    + eapply lc_ty_mono; eauto.
    + eapply IHt; eauto. lia.
  - apply lc_tm_app; [eapply IHt1; eauto | eapply IHt2; eauto].
  - apply lc_tm_tabs. eapply IHt; eauto.
  - apply lc_tm_tapp; [eapply IHt; eauto | eapply lc_ty_mono; eauto].
  - apply lc_tm_choice; [eapply IHt1; eauto | eapply IHt2; eauto].
Qed.
```
Note the abs case goal K' <= ... lc_tm_abs K' k' (abs T t) requires lc_ty_at K' T and lc_tm_at K' (S k') t. `eapply lc_tm_abs` gives goals lc_ty_at K' T and lc_tm_at K' (S k') t. eapply lc_ty_mono with (k:=K) — need to instantiate; `eapply lc_ty_mono; eauto` might leave side goals K<=K' ✓ auto. For the body, IHt with K,k,K',S k'; need lc_tm_at K' (S k') t: eapply IHt; eauto; lia. Good, but eauto might pick wrong. We'll see.

Also need monotonicity for lc_ty with k parameter only.

Type elim:
```
Lemma lc_ty_open_elim : forall T k U K,
  lc_ty_at K (open_ty_rec k U T) -> lc_ty_at (S K) T.
Proof.
  induction T; intros k U K H; simpl in H.
  - (* bvar n *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. apply lc_ty_bvar. lia.
    + inversion H; subst. apply lc_ty_bvar. lia.
      (* H : lc_ty_at K (bvar n) gives n < K; need n < S K *)
  - apply lc_ty_fvar.
  - inversion H; subst. apply lc_ty_arrow; [apply IHT1; assumption | apply IHT2; assumption].
  - inversion H; subst. apply lc_ty_all. eapply IHT. eassumption.
```
Wait, for bvar and eqb false: H : lc_ty_at K (Ty_BVar n) after simpl? The LHS after simpl for T=bvar n is `lc_ty_at K (if Nat.eqb k n then U else Ty_BVar n)`; if eqb false, H : lc_ty_at K (Ty_BVar n). Inversion gives n<K. Need n<S K ✓ lia.
For eqb true: H : lc_ty_at K U; need lc_ty_at (S K) (Ty_BVar n) with n=k: k<S K ✓ lia. Note after subst n, the goal is lc_ty_at (S K) (Ty_BVar k): k < S K ✓.
For All: after simpl, H : lc_ty_at K (Ty_All (open_ty_rec (S k) U T)); inversion gives lc_ty_at (S K) (open_ty_rec (S k) U T); apply IHT with k:=S k, U, K:=S K; assumption. Then goal lc_ty_at (S K) (Ty_All T) needs lc_ty_at (S (S K)) T; IHT with K:=S K gives lc_ty_at (S (S K)) T ✓.
So `apply lc_ty_all; eapply IHT; eassumption.` Wait `apply lc_ty_all` changes goal to lc_ty_at (S (S K)) T. `eapply IHT` with instantiations K:=S K, k:=S k gives conclusion lc_ty_at (S (S K)) T from hypothesis lc_ty_at (S K) (open_ty_rec (S k) U T) ✓.

Type intro:
```
Lemma lc_ty_open_intro : forall T K U,
  lc_ty_at (S K) T -> lc_ty_at K U -> lc_ty_at K (open_ty_rec K U T).
Proof.
  induction T; intros K U H HU; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb K n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n. exact HU.
    + apply lc_ty_bvar. inversion H. lia.  (* H : lc_ty_at (S K) (bvar n) -> n < S K; eqb false -> n≠K; so n<K *)
  - apply lc_ty_fvar.
  - inversion H; subst. apply lc_ty_arrow; [apply IHT1 | apply IHT2]; auto.
  - inversion H; subst. apply lc_ty_all. eapply IHT. eassumption. eapply lc_ty_mono; eauto.
```
For All: H : lc_ty_at (S K) (Ty_All T); inversion gives H1 : lc_ty_at (S (S K)) T. Goal lc_ty_at K (Ty_All (open_ty_rec (S K) U T)) → apply lc_ty_all → lc_ty_at (S K) (open_ty_rec (S K) U T). Apply IHT with K:=S K, U: needs lc_ty_at (S (S K)) T ✓ H1, and lc_ty_at (S K) U from HU by mono (K ≤ S K) ✓.
For Arrow: H inversion gives lc_ty_at (S K) T1, T2; IHT1 with K,U gives lc_ty_at K (open_ty_rec K U T1) ✓.
Base bvar eqb false: H : lc_ty_at (S K) (Ty_BVar n) gives n < S K; need n < K. We know n≠K; n<S K means n≤K; with n≠K, n<K ✓ lia.

Qed.

```
Lemma wf_ty_lc : forall Delta T, wf_ty Delta T -> lc_ty_at 0 T.
Proof.
  intros Delta T H. induction H.
  - apply lc_ty_fvar.
  - apply lc_ty_arrow; assumption.
  - (* WF_All *) apply lc_ty_all. 
    (* H : forall X, ~In X L -> wf_ty (X::Delta) (open_ty T (Ty_FVar X)) *)
    (* IH : forall X, ~In X L -> lc_ty_at 0 (open_ty T (Ty_FVar X)) *)
    pose proof (IH (fresh L) (fresh_not_in L)) as Hc.
    eapply lc_ty_open_elim. eassumption.
Qed.
```
Wait, in the WF_All case, the constructor's subderivation is a function; the induction principle gives IH : forall X, ~In X L -> lc_ty_at 0 (open_ty T (Ty_FVar X))? The hypothesis of WF_All is `forall X, ~ In X L -> wf_ty (X::Delta) (open_ty T (Ty_FVar X))`. Induction on H with the motive `fun Delta T H => lc_ty_at 0 T` will generate IH as forall X, ~In X L -> lc_ty_at 0 (open_ty T (Ty_FVar X))? Since the motive ignores Delta, yes the IH for the premise which varies with X should be forall X, .... Let's hope. Note `open_ty T (Ty_FVar X)` is open_ty_rec 0; lc_ty_open_elim with K=0, k=0 gives lc_ty_at 1 T; then lc_ty_all. ✓.

has_type_lc: mutual with using wf_ty_lc and the opening lemmas.
```
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T -> lc_tm_at 0 0 t /\ lc_ty_at 0 T.
Proof.
  intros Delta Gamma t T H. induction H.
  - (* T_Var *) split; [apply lc_tm_fvar | apply wf_ty_lc; assumption].
  - (* T_Abs *) split.
    + apply lc_tm_abs.
      * apply wf_ty_lc; assumption.
      * (* IH : forall x, ~In x L -> lc_tm_at 0 0 (open_tm t2 (tm_fvar x)) /\ lc_ty_at 0 T2 *)
        pose proof (IH (fresh L) (fresh_not_in L)) as [Ht _].
        simpl in Ht. eapply lc_tm_open_tm_elim. eassumption.
    + apply lc_ty_arrow; [apply wf_ty_lc; assumption | apply (proj2 (IH (fresh L) (fresh_not_in L)))].
  - (* T_App *) split.
    + apply lc_tm_app; [apply (proj1 IH1) | apply (proj1 IH2)].
    + destruct IH1 as [_ Hty]. inversion Hty; subst. assumption.
  - (* T_TAbs *) split.
    + apply lc_tm_tabs.
      pose proof (IH (fresh L) (fresh_not_in L)) as [Ht _].
      simpl in Ht. eapply lc_tm_open_ty_elim; [lia | eassumption].
    + apply lc_ty_all.
      pose proof (IH (fresh L) (fresh_not_in L)) as [_ Hty].
      simpl in Hty. eapply lc_ty_open_elim. eassumption.
  - (* T_TApp *) split.
    + apply lc_tm_tapp.
      * apply (proj1 IH).
      * apply wf_ty_lc; assumption.
    + (* result type open_ty T U = open_ty_rec 0 U T *)
      apply (proj2 IH) in ... 
```

```
      destruct IH as [_ Hty]. inversion Hty; subst.
      eapply lc_ty_open_intro; [eassumption | apply wf_ty_lc; assumption].
```

Now, the semantic part. Let me define VR/TR mutually. In Coq, mutual Fixpoints over an indexed inductive with decreasing on T. Syntax:

```coq
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => lookup_rel benv i v
  ...
  end
with TR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed_tm t /\ exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ VR T benv rho v.
```
`lookup_rel` must be defined before. PRel definition:
```
Definition PRel := tm -> Prop.
```
But `PRel` is a function type; using it in a Fixpoint's return type is fine.

Wait, VR's let-binding of `lookup_rel` defined as a function returning PRel:
```
Definition lookup_rel (benv : list PRel) (i : nat) : PRel :=
  match nth_error benv i with Some P => P | None => fun _ => False end.
```
Then VR BVar: `lookup_rel benv i v`.

Now, the mutual fixpoint termination: In TR, `VR T benv rho v` is called with the same T as TR's argument, which is a parameter, not a subterm of something. Coq's guard checker for mutual fixpoints requires calls to be on structurally smaller arguments relative to the caller's own argument. TR T ... calls VR T ... with the same T — for mutual recursion, this is allowed? The guard condition: in a mutually recursive definition, calls between functions must be on arguments that are structurally smaller than the caller's corresponding argument? Actually, the guard checker for mutual fixpoints requires that recursive calls (including mutual) have a decreasing argument. If TR calls VR with the same T, and VR calls TR with T1 < T, then there's a cycle TR T -> VR T -> TR T1 (smaller)... The guard checker might reject because TR T -> VR T is not decreasing (same argument). In Coq, mutual fixpoints allow calls with a smaller or equal argument? The standard restriction: recursive calls must be on a strict subterm of one of the arguments of the *same* function? For mutual fixpoints, the decreasing argument must be the same position across the block; a call to another function in the block must be on a structurally smaller argument than the caller's decreasing argument. So TR T calling VR T with equal T would violate.

Alternative: define TR in terms of VR without mutual recursion by making TR a Definition after VR? But VR's arrow/all clauses mention TR. We can define a single Fixpoint with a "mode" or define both as a single function returning a pair? Or define VR and TR where TR is a *local* definition inside VR? Since TR T = lc ∧ ∃v, path ∧ VR T v, and VR calls TR on subterms. We can inline TR: in VR's arrow clause, `TR T2 ... (app v w)` unfolds to lc ∧ ∃v', ... VR T2. We can define a *single* Fixpoint `VR` where occurrences of TR are expanded:

```
Fixpoint VR T benv rho v :=
  match T with
  | Arrow T1 T2 => forall w, value w -> VR T1 benv rho w ->
      (locally_closed_tm (tm_app v w) /\ exists v', tm_app v w -->* v' /\ value v' /\ VR T2 benv rho v')
  ...
```
But then we'd need TR notation; we can define `TR T benv rho t := lc t /\ exists v, ... VR T ...` as a Definition after VR, and in VR's clauses use the expanded form `TR_def T2 ...` — but that's exactly the same expression; can we refer to a Definition defined later? No.

Alternative: define VR first with a parameter for the "continuation" relation? Use a nested fixpoint on T with TR defined inside? In Coq, we can define:

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => lookup_rel benv i v
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X v
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      forall w, value w -> VR T1 benv rho w ->
        (locally_closed_tm (tm_app v w) /\ exists v', tm_app v w -->* v' /\ value v' /\ VR T2 benv rho v')
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      forall U P, locally_closed_ty U ->
        (locally_closed_tm (tm_tapp v U) /\ exists v', tm_tapp v U -->* v' /\ value v' /\ VR T1 (P::benv) rho v')
  end.
Definition TR T benv rho t := locally_closed_tm t /\ exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ VR T benv rho v.
```
This works structurally: VR calls itself on T1/T2 subterms. The expansions are exactly TR's definition. Then lemmas about TR can be stated via the definition; when unfolding VR at Arrow, we get the expanded TR form, but if we have a goal `TR T2 ... (app v w)` defined as lc ∧ ∃..., the expansion matches definitionally (after unfold TR). So we can write proofs using `unfold TR` to match.

Alternatively, define TR as a Definition and VR using a helper `TRb T benv rho t := locally_closed_tm t /\ exists v, ... VR ...` — but VR must be defined first. Since TR is just a notation for the expansion, using the expansion inside VR and defining TR after works. Proofs: when VR Arrow is unfolded, the goal is exactly `TR T2 benv rho (app v w)` after `unfold TR` (definitional equality should hold: TR T2 benv rho (app v w) unfolds to lc ∧ exists..., which is syntactically what's in VR's branch). Yes, definitional equality holds, so `change (TR T2 benv rho (tm_app v w))` works.

Let's do that. Define:
```
Definition PRel := tm -> Prop.
Definition lookup_rel ...
Fixpoint VR T benv rho v := match ... end.
Definition TR T benv rho t := locally_closed_tm t /\ exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ VR T benv rho v.
```

Now, the substitution lemma VR_open_ty_rec_subst: statement with VR; proof by induction on T; uses TR equivalence via `TR_congr`. TR_congr:
```
Lemma TR_congr : forall T1 T2 benv1 benv2 rho1 rho2 t,
  (forall v, VR T1 benv1 rho1 v <-> VR T2 benv2 rho2 v) ->
  (TR T1 benv1 rho1 t <-> TR T2 benv2 rho2 t).
Proof.
  intros. unfold TR. split; intros [Hlc [v [Hs [Hval Hv]]]];
    split; [exact Hlc|]; exists v; split; [exact Hs|]; split; [exact Hval|];
    [apply H | apply H]; exact Hv.
Qed.
```
Good.

Now the type-level lookup lemmas:
```
Lemma lookup_rel_app_single_eq : forall l P v,
  lookup_rel (l ++ [P]) (length l) v <-> P v.
Proof.
  intro l. induction l as [| a l IH]; intros P v; simpl.
  - simpl. reflexivity. (* lookup_rel [P] 0 v = P v *)
  - (* (a::l)++[P] = a :: (l++[P]); length (a::l) = S (length l) *)
    simpl. apply IH.
Qed.
```
Careful: `length (a::l)` is S (length l); `lookup_rel ((a::l)++[P]) (S (length l)) v` simplifies to lookup_rel (l++[P]) (length l) v. And P v on the right. ✓.
```
Lemma lookup_rel_app_single_lt : forall l P i v,
  i < length l -> (lookup_rel (l ++ [P]) i v <-> lookup_rel l i v).
Proof.
  intro l. induction l as [| a l IH]; intros P i v Hi; simpl in *.
  - lia.
  - destruct i.
    + simpl. reflexivity.
    + simpl. apply IH. lia.
Qed.
```
```
Lemma lookup_rel_app_single_gt : forall l P i v,
  length l < i -> (lookup_rel (l ++ [P]) i v <-> False).
Proof.
  intro l. induction l as [| a l IH]; intros P i v Hi; simpl in *.
  - destruct i; [lia|]. simpl. reflexivity? 
```

Now, the fundamental theorem. Let's write its statement:

```
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (rho : atom -> PRel) (gamma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T -> value (gamma x) /\ VR T [] rho (gamma x).

Lemma fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma rho gamma,
    (forall X, locally_closed_ty (sigma X)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma x)) ->
    env_rel Gamma rho gamma ->
    TR T [] rho (apply_env sigma gamma t).
Proof.
  intros Delta Gamma t T H. induction H; intros sigma rho gamma Hsig Hgam Henv.
  ...
```
Induction on H with sigma,rho,gamma universally quantified after. But the env_rel and sigma/gamma hypotheses involve Delta/Gamma; after induction, the IH for subderivations are `forall sigma rho gamma, Hsig -> Hgam -> env_rel Gamma' rho gamma -> TR T' [] rho (apply ...)`. Good.

Define:
```
Lemma apply_env_lc : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma gamma,
    (forall X, locally_closed_ty (sigma X)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma x)) ->
    locally_closed_tm (apply_env sigma gamma t).
Proof.
  intros Delta Gamma t T H. 
  pose proof (has_type_lc _ _ _ _ H) as [Hlc Hlcty].
  intros sigma gamma Hsig Hgam.
  unfold apply_env.
  apply tm_subst_env_lc with (gamma:=gamma).
  - exact Hgam.  (* lc at 0 0 *)
  - apply tm_ty_subst_env_lc with (sigma:=sigma).
    + exact Hsig.
    + exact Hlc.
Qed.

```coq
Lemma fold_max_upper : forall l x, In x l -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; simpl; intros x Hx.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (fold_right Nat.max 0 l); [apply IH; exact Hx | apply Nat.le_max_r].

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.

  intros l H. unfold fresh in H.
  pose proof (fold_max_upper l (S (fold_right Nat.max 0 l)) H). lia.
Qed.

(* ---------------- Substitution commutes with opening ---------------- *)

Lemma ty_subst_env_open_ty_rec : forall sigma,
  (forall X, locally_closed_ty (sigma X)) ->
  forall k U T,
    ty_subst_env sigma (open_ty_rec k U T) =
    open_ty_rec k (ty_subst_env sigma U) (ty_subst_env sigma T).
Proof.
  intros sigma Hsig k U T. revert k U. induction T; intros k U; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n); reflexivity.
  - symmetry. apply open_ty_rec_id_lc. apply Hsig. lia.
  - f_equal; [apply IHT1 | apply IHT2].
  - f_equal. apply IHT.

Better: avoid inversion naming by proving monotonicity via induction on the derivation H with the induction principle and generalized indices:

```
Lemma lc_tm_mono : forall t K k K' k',
  lc_tm_at K k t -> K <= K' -> k <= k' -> lc_tm_at K' k' t.
Proof.
  intros t K k K' k' H. revert K' k'.
  induction H; intros K' k' HK Hk.
```
Here `induction H` with H : lc_tm_at K k t; since K,k,t are variables (introduced), Coq's induction will generalize them as indices. The IHs will be:
- For lc_tm_abs: IH : forall K' k', K <= K' -> (S k) <= k' -> lc_tm_at K' k' t. To prove lc_tm_at K' (S k') t, need (S k) <= (S k') from k≤k' ✓.
- For lc_ty_at premises (non-recursive), no IH.

Definitions:

```coq
(* ================================================================ *)
(* Semantic logical relation (unary logical predicate)               *)
(* ================================================================ *)

Definition PRel := tm -> Prop.

Definition lookup_rel (benv : list PRel) (i : nat) : PRel :=
  match nth_error benv i with
  | Some P => P
  | None => fun _ => False
  end.

Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => lookup_rel benv i v
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X v
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      forall w, value w -> VR T1 benv rho w ->
        locally_closed_tm (tm_app v w) /\
        (exists v', tm_app v w -->* v' /\ value v' /\ VR T2 benv rho v')
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      forall U P, locally_closed_ty U ->
        locally_closed_tm (tm_tapp v U) /\
        (exists v', tm_tapp v U -->* v' /\ value v' /\ VR T1 (P :: benv) rho v')
  end.

Definition TR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed_tm t /\ exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ VR T benv rho v.
```

Note that in VR Arrow/All I inlined TR. Then TR is definitionally equal to lc ∧ exists ... for the same T. But is `TR T2 benv rho (tm_app v w)` definitionally equal to the inline expression? TR T2 benv rho (app v w) unfolds to `locally_closed_tm (app v w) /\ exists v', app v w -->* v' /\ value v' /\ VR T2 benv rho v'`. The inline expression in VR is exactly that ✓. So `fold TR` / `change` works.

For uniformity in proofs, I can use `change (TR T2 benv rho (tm_app v w))` when needed. Similarly All.

Let's write:
```coq
Definition PRel := tm -> Prop.

Definition lookup_rel (benv : list PRel) (i : nat) : PRel :=
  match nth_error benv i with
  | Some P => P
  | None => fun _ => False
  end.

Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_BVar i => lookup_rel benv i v
  | Ty_FVar X => rho X v
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      forall w, value w -> VR T1 benv rho w ->
        locally_closed_tm (tm_app v w) /\
        (exists v', tm_app v w -->* v' /\ value v' /\ VR T2 benv rho v')
  | Ty_All T1 =>
      forall U P, locally_closed_ty U ->
        locally_closed_tm (tm_tapp v U) /\
        (exists v', tm_tapp v U -->* v' /\ value v' /\ VR T1 (P :: benv) rho v')
  end.

Definition TR (T : ty) (benv : list PRel) (rho : atom -> PRel) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed_tm t /\ exists v, t -->* v /\ value v /\ VR T benv rho v.
```

```coq
Lemma multi_trans : forall a b c, a -->* b -> b -->* c -> a -->* c.
Proof.
  intros a b c H1 H2. revert c H2. induction H1; intros c H2.
  - exact H2.
  - eapply multi_step; [exact H | apply IHH1; exact H2].
Qed.
```
Here `induction H1` with H1 : multi a b and goal forall c, multi b c -> multi a c. In the step case, H : a --> y? The constructor multi_step x y z : x --> y -> multi y z -> multi x z. After induction, name hypotheses Hxy, Hyz, IHyz. The pattern: `induction H1` generates names; `eapply multi_step; [exact H | apply IHH1; exact H2]` might use wrong names. Let's write with `induction H1 as [x | x y z Hxy Hyz IH]`.
```
Lemma multi_trans : forall a b c, a -->* b -> b -->* c -> a -->* c.
Proof.
  intros a b c H1 H2. revert c H2.
  induction H1 as [x | x y z Hxy Hyz IH]; intros c H2.
  - exact H2.
  - eapply multi_step. exact Hxy. apply IH. exact H2.
Qed.
```
Hmm, in the step case, b = z, and IH : forall c, multi z c -> multi y c. Goal multi x c. eapply multi_step with y? `eapply multi_step` should pick x,y,z? The goal multi x c; multi_step : multi u v -> multi v w -> multi u w. Unify u=x, w=c; leaves multi x ?v and multi ?v c. The first can be Hxy with ?v=y; then goal multi y c solved by IH H2. `eapply multi_step; [exact Hxy | apply IH; exact H2]` ✓.

TR lemmas:
```
Lemma TR_lc : forall T benv rho t, TR T benv rho t -> locally_closed_tm t.
Proof. intros. exact (proj1 H). Qed.

Lemma TR_congr : forall T1 T2 benv1 benv2 rho1 rho2 t,
  (forall v, VR T1 benv1 rho1 v <-> VR T2 benv2 rho2 v) ->
  (TR T1 benv1 rho1 t <-> TR T2 benv2 rho2 t).
Proof.
  intros. unfold TR. split; intros [Hlc [v [Hs [Hval Hvr]]]];
    split; [exact Hlc | exists v; split; [exact Hs | split; [exact Hval |]]];
    [apply H | apply H]; exact Hvr.
Qed.
```
Wait, for the reverse direction the `apply H` is used with Hvr : VR T2 benv2 rho2 v but goal VR T1 benv1 rho1 v; `apply H` where H : forall v, VR T1 ... ↔ VR T2 ...; to prove the reverse goal, use `apply H`? `apply H` with iff applies the direction matching the goal, i.e., it needs VR T1 ↔ VR T2 and goal VR T1? Hmm. For the reverse, goal is VR T1 b1 v and H is an iff; `apply H` transforms goal VR T1 into VR T2 (using the backward direction of the iff: to prove A from B, apply the iff if the goal is A and the lemma is A ↔ B? `apply` with an iff uses the direction that turns the goal into the other side if possible: for goal A, apply (A↔B) gives goal B. Yes). So both directions use `apply H`? Let's test: In direction 1, goal VR T2, Hvr : VR T1; `apply H` where H : VR T1 ↔ VR T2; applying to goal VR T2 needs the reverse direction: gives goal VR T1 ✓. In direction 2, goal VR T1, Hvr : VR T2; apply H with goal VR T1 gives goal VR T2 ✓. So `apply H` works for both? Actually `apply (proj1 H)` for direction 1 and `apply (proj2 H)` for direction 2, but plain `apply H` may work both ways depending on unification. To be safe, write two separate branches explicitly rather than a combined `[...|...]`:
```
  intros Ht. unfold TR. split.
  - intros [Hlc [v [Hs [Hval Hvr]]]]. split; [exact Hlc|]. exists v. split; [exact Hs|]. split; [exact Hval|]. apply H. exact Hvr.
  - intros [Hlc [v [Hs [Hval Hvr]]]]. split; [exact Hlc|]. exists v. split; [exact Hs|]. split; [exact Hval|]. apply H. exact Hvr.
Qed.
```
`apply H` in direction 1: goal VR T2 b2 rho2 v, Hvr : VR T1 b1 rho1 v, H : forall v, VR T1 ↔ VR T2. `apply H` should use proj2 (VR T1 -> VR T2) to prove VR T2 from VR T1 ✓. In direction 2, goal VR T1, Hvr : VR T2; apply H uses proj1? Wait proj1 : VR T1 -> VR T2, not helpful to prove VR T1 from VR T2. `apply H` with goal VR T1 will match the conclusion of the iff's... Applying an iff lemma A↔B to goal A: if the goal matches B (the RHS), then it leaves A; if it matches A, leaves B. Goal VR T1, and H's RHS is VR T2, LHS VR T1. Goal matches LHS, so apply uses the ← direction, leaving RHS VR T2 ✓. Good.

Lemma TR_multi : forall T benv rho t u,

Lemma VR_benv_agree : forall k T, lc_ty_at k T ->

VR_benv_pequiv: similar, induction on T (structural) or on lc? No lc hypothesis. Do induction on T with the benv_pequiv hypothesis:
```
Lemma VR_benv_pequiv : forall T benv1 benv2 rho v,
  benv_pequiv benv1 benv2 -> (VR T benv1 rho v <-> VR T benv2 rho v).
Proof.
  induction T; intros benv1 benv2 rho v Hpe.
  - simpl. apply Hpe.
  - simpl. reflexivity.
  - simpl. split; intros H w Hw Harg.
    + apply (proj1 (TR_congr _ _ _ _ _ _ (fun v' => IHT2 benv1 benv2 rho v' Hpe))).
      apply H with (w:=w); [exact Hw | apply (proj2 (IHT1 benv1 benv2 rho w Hpe)); exact Harg].
    + apply (proj2 (TR_congr _ _ _ _ _ _ (fun v' => IHT2 benv1 benv2 rho v' Hpe))).
      apply H with (w:=w); [exact Hw | apply (proj1 (IHT1 benv1 benv2 rho w Hpe)); exact Harg].
  - simpl. split; intros H U P HlcU.
    + apply (proj1 (TR_congr _ _ _ _ _ _ (fun v' => IHT1 (P::benv1) (P::benv2) rho v' (benv_pequiv_cons Hpe P)))).
      apply H; exact HlcU.
    + apply (proj2 (TR_congr _ _ _ _ _ _ (fun v' => IHT1 (P::benv1) (P::benv2) rho v' (benv_pequiv_cons Hpe P)))).
      apply H; exact HlcU.
Qed.
```
Here IHT1 is the IH for T1, universally quantified over benv1 benv2 rho v? Since induction on T with benv1,... introduced after? If we write `induction T; intros benv1 benv2 rho v Hpe`, the IHs are `forall benv1 benv2 rho v, benv_pequiv b1 b2 -> ...` per subterm ✓.
Note in the All case, IHT1 applies to T1 with benv (P::benv1) etc. ✓.

benv_pequiv_cons : benv_pequiv b1 b2 -> benv_pequiv (P::b1) (P::b2). Proof: intros Hpe i v; destruct i; simpl; [reflexivity | apply Hpe].

benv_agree_sym : benv_agree k b1 b2 -> benv_agree k b2 b1. Proof: intros H i Hi v; symmetry; apply H; assumption.
benv_agree_cons : benv_agree k b1 b2 -> benv_agree (S k) (P::b1) (P::b2). Proof: intros H i Hi v; destruct i; simpl; [reflexivity | apply H; lia].

VR_rho_agree:
```
Lemma VR_rho_agree : forall T benv rho1 rho2,
  (forall X, In X (fv_ty T) -> forall v, rho1 X v <-> rho2 X v) ->
  forall v, VR T benv rho1 v <-> VR T benv rho2 v.
Proof.
  induction T; intros benv rho1 rho2 Hagree v; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply Hagree. simpl. auto.
  - split; intros H w Hw Harg.
    + apply (proj1 (TR_congr _ _ _ _ _ _ (fun v' => IHT2 benv rho1 rho2 Hagree2 v'))).
      apply H with (w:=w); [exact Hw | apply (proj2 (IHT1 benv rho1 rho2 Hagree1 w)); exact Harg].
    + apply (proj2 (TR_congr _ _ _ _ _ _ (fun v' => IHT2 benv rho1 rho2 Hagree2 v'))).
      apply H with (w:=w); [exact Hw | apply (proj1 (IHT1 benv rho1 rho2 Hagree1 w)); exact Harg].
  - split; intros H U P HlcU.
    + apply (proj1 (TR_congr _ _ _ _ _ _ (fun v' => IHT1 (P::benv) rho1 rho2 Hagree1 v'))).
      apply H; exact HlcU.
    + apply (proj2 ...); apply H; exact HlcU.
Qed.
```

Substitution lemma:

```
Lemma VR_open_ty_rec_subst : forall T j benv rho U,
  length benv = j ->
  locally_closed_ty U ->
  lc_ty_at (S j) T ->
  forall v,
    VR (open_ty_rec j U T) benv rho v <->
    VR T (benv ++ [VR U benv rho]) rho v.
Proof.
  induction T; intros j benv rho U Hlen HU Hlc v.
  - (* BVar n *)
    simpl. inversion Hlc; subst.
    destruct (Nat.eqb j n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n.
      rewrite Hlen. apply lookup_rel_app_single_eq.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
      apply lookup_rel_app_single_lt.
      rewrite Hlen. 
      (* need n < j: from n < S j and n ≠ j *)
      destruct (Nat.lt_ge_cases n j) as [Hlt | Hge]; [exact Hlt |].
      exfalso. assert (Hle : n <= j) by (apply Nat.lt_succ_r; assumption).
      assert (Heq : n = j) by (apply Nat.le_antisymm; assumption).
      apply E. exact Heq.
```
Careful: after `simpl`, the goal is `(if Nat.eqb j n then VR U benv rho v else lookup_rel benv n v) ↔ lookup_rel (benv++[VR U benv rho]) n v`. Actually VR (open_ty_rec j U (bvar n)) = VR (if eqb j n then U else bvar n) = if eqb ... then VR U benv rho v else lookup_rel benv n v. RHS = lookup_rel (benv++[R]) n v. With destruct eqb:
- true: goal VR U benv rho v ↔ lookup_rel (benv++[R]) n v with n=j and Hlen: length benv = j. So rewrite Hlen turns n = length benv; lookup_rel_app_single_eq gives lookup_rel (l++[P]) (length l) v ↔ P v where P = VR U benv rho ✓.
- false: goal lookup_rel benv n v ↔ lookup_rel (benv++[R]) n v; apply lookup_rel_app_single_lt with n < length benv. Need n < j and rewrite Hlen. From inversion Hlc: n < S j; E : (j =? n) = false → j ≠ n. n ≠ j. Using the case analysis as before.
The `exfalso` approach: to prove n < length benv, first rewrite Hlen, then show n<j. We can do:
```
      assert (Hn : n < j).
      { destruct (Nat.lt_ge_cases n j) as [Hlt | Hge]; [exact Hlt |].
        exfalso. assert (Hle : n <= j) by (apply Nat.lt_succ_r; assumption).
        assert (Heq : n = j) by (apply Nat.le_antisymm; assumption).
        apply E. symmetry. exact Heq. }
      rewrite Hlen. apply lookup_rel_app_single_lt. exact Hn.
```
Wait, `apply lookup_rel_app_single_lt` with goal `lookup_rel benv n v ↔ lookup_rel (benv++[R]) n v`? The lemma states: i < length l -> (lookup_rel (l++[P]) i v ↔ lookup_rel l i v). The goal orientation is lookup_rel benv n v ↔ lookup_rel (benv++[R]) n v (LHS is open result, RHS is the substituted type). Hmm, direction: our goal is `lookup_rel benv n v ↔ lookup_rel (benv ++ [R]) n v`. lookup_rel_app_single_lt gives `lookup_rel (benv++[R]) n v ↔ lookup_rel benv n v`. So apply gives the reverse; use `apply iff_sym` or `symmetry; apply lookup_rel_app_single_lt`. Let's define the lemma with the order matching our goal: `lookup_rel l i v <-> lookup_rel (l++[P]) i v`. ```
Lemma lookup_rel_app_single_lt : forall l P i v,

```

Proofs:
```
Lemma lookup_rel_app_single_lt : forall l P i v,
  i < length l -> (lookup_rel l i v <-> lookup_rel (l ++ [P]) i v).
Proof.
  intro l. induction l as [| a l IH]; intros P i v Hi; simpl in *.
  - lia.
  - destruct i.
    + simpl. reflexivity.
    + simpl. apply IH. lia.
Qed.
```
Check: l = a::l. (a::l)++[P] = a::(l++[P]); length (a::l) = S (length l). i=0: lookup_rel (a::l) 0 v = a v; lookup_rel (a::(l++[P])) 0 v = a v ✓. i=S i': lookup_rel (a::l) (S i') v = lookup_rel l i' v; RHS lookup_rel (a::(l++[P])) (S i') v = lookup_rel (l++[P]) i' v; apply IH with i'<length l ✓.

```
Lemma lookup_rel_app_single_eq : forall l P v,
  P v <-> lookup_rel (l ++ [P]) (length l) v.
Proof.
  intro l. induction l as [| a l IH]; intros P v; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply IH.
Qed.
```
Check l=[]: ([]++[P]) = [P], length 0; lookup_rel [P] 0 v = P v ✓.
l=a::l: (a::l)++[P] = a::(l++[P]); length (a::l) = S (length l); lookup_rel (a::(l++[P])) (S (length l)) v = lookup_rel (l++[P]) (length l) v; IH ✓.

Now write the substitution lemma:

```coq
Lemma VR_open_ty_rec_subst : forall T j benv rho U,
  length benv = j ->
  locally_closed_ty U ->
  lc_ty_at (S j) T ->
  forall v,
    VR (open_ty_rec j U T) benv rho v <->
    VR T (benv ++ [VR U benv rho]) rho v.
Proof.
  induction T as [ n | X | T1 IH1 T2 IH2 | T1 IH1 ];
    intros j benv rho U Hlen HU Hlc v.
  - (* BVar n *) simpl.
    assert (HnSj : n < S j) by (inversion Hlc; assumption).
    destruct (Nat.eqb j n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst n.
      rewrite Hlen. apply lookup_rel_app_single_eq.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E.
      assert (Hn : n < j).
      { destruct (Nat.lt_ge_cases n j) as [Hlt | Hge]; [exact Hlt |].
        exfalso.
        assert (Hle : n <= j) by (apply Nat.lt_succ_r; assumption).
        assert (Heq : n = j) by (apply Nat.le_antisymm; assumption).
        apply E. exact Heq. }
      rewrite Hlen. apply lookup_rel_app_single_lt. exact Hn.
  - simpl. reflexivity.
  - (* Arrow *) simpl.
    inversion Hlc; subst.
    split; intros H w Hw Harg.
    + change (TR T2 (benv ++ [VR U benv rho]) rho (tm_app v w)).
      apply (proj1 (TR_congr (open_ty_rec j U T2) T2 benv (benv ++ [VR U benv rho]) rho rho
                    (tm_app v w) (fun v' => IH2 j benv rho U Hlen HU H2 v'))).
      apply H with (w := w).
      * exact Hw.
      * apply (proj2 (IH1 j benv rho U Hlen HU H1 w)). exact Harg.
    + change (TR (open_ty_rec j U T2) benv rho (tm_app v w)).
      apply (proj2 (TR_congr (open_ty_rec j U T2) T2 benv (benv ++ [VR U benv rho]) rho rho
                    (tm_app v w) (fun v' => IH2 j benv rho U Hlen HU H2 v'))).
      apply H with (w := w).
      * exact Hw.
      * apply (proj1 (IH1 j benv rho U Hlen HU H1 w)). exact Harg.
  - (* All *) simpl.
    inversion Hlc; subst.
    assert (Hlen' : length (P :: benv) = S j) by (simpl; rewrite Hlen; reflexivity).
    split; intros H U2 P HlcU2.
    + change (TR T1 (P :: (benv ++ [VR U benv rho])) rho (tm_tapp v U2)).
      apply (proj1 (TR_congr (open_ty_rec (S j) U T1) T1 (P::benv) (P::(benv++[VR U benv rho])) rho rho
                    (tm_tapp v U2) (fun v' => Hpt v'))).
      apply H; exact HlcU2.
    + change (TR (open_ty_rec (S j) U T1) (P::benv) rho (tm_tapp v U2)).
      apply (proj2 (TR_congr ... same Hpt ...)).
      apply H; exact HlcU2.
```

The substitution lemma compiles. Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write it.

Statement:
```coq
Definition env_rel (Gamma : context) (rho : atom -> PRel) (gamma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, lookup_context x Gamma = Some T ->
    value (gamma x) /\ VR T [] rho (gamma x).

Lemma fundamental : forall Delta Gamma t T,
  has_type Delta Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma rho gamma,
    (forall X, locally_closed_ty (sigma X)) ->
    (forall x, locally_closed_tm (gamma x)) ->
    env_rel Gamma rho gamma ->
    TR T [] rho (apply_env sigma gamma t).
Proof.
  intros Delta Gamma t T H.
  induction H as
    [ Delta Gamma x T Hlookup Hwf
    | L Delta Gamma T1 t2 T2 HwfT1 Hbody IHbody
    | Delta Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
    | L Delta Gamma t T Hbody IHbody
    | Delta Gamma t T U Ht IH HwfU
    | Delta Gamma t1 t2 T Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2 ];
    intros sigma rho gamma Hsig Hgam Henv.
```

T_TApp:
Goal after simpl: TR (open_ty T U) [] rho (tm_tapp (apply_env σ γ t) (ty_subst_env σ U)).
```
  - (* T_TApp *)
    simpl.
    change (TR (open_ty T U) [] rho (tm_tapp (apply_env sigma gamma t) (ty_subst_env sigma U))).

```
Wait, Hvr's type: VR (Ty_All T) [] rho v = forall U' P, lc U' -> inline TR T (P::[]) rho (tapp v U'). To get TR T [VR U [] rho] rho (tapp v (σU)), we apply Hvr to U' := σU, P := VR U [] rho. The conclusion is the inline TR which is definitionally TR T [P] [] (tapp v σU)? The inline is `locally_closed_tm (tapp v U') /\ exists ... VR T (P::[]) ...`; TR T [P] [] ... is definitionally the same ✓. But `apply Hvr` may need the term to match: goal TR T [VR U [] rho] rho (tapp v (σU)); Hvr applied to HlcU yields exactly that (definitionally). Let's use:
```
    assert (HtrT : TR T [VR U [] rho] rho (tm_tapp v (ty_subst_env sigma U))).
    { apply Hvr. exact HlcU. }
```
Hmm, Hvr : forall (U':ty)(P:PRel), lc U' -> inline. `apply Hvr` with goal TR ... will unify the inline with TR (conversion) and leave goals lc (σU) and P? P is inferred from the goal's benv [VR U [] rho] matching P::[] → P = VR U [] rho ✓. U' from tapp v (σU) matching tapp v U' → U'=σU ✓. So `apply Hvr; exact HlcU` works.
Then:
```
    (* convert TR T [P] to TR (open_ty T U) [] via substitution lemma *)
    assert (Hpt : forall v', VR (open_ty T U) [] rho v' <-> VR T [VR U [] rho] rho v').
    { intro v'. apply (VR_open_ty_rec_subst T 0 [] rho U).
      - reflexivity.  (* length [] = 0 *)
      - eapply wf_ty_lc; eassumption.  (* locally_closed_ty U *)
      - simpl. exact HlcT. }
```
Wait, VR_open_ty_rec_subst T j benv rho U : length benv = j -> lc U -> lc_ty_at (S j) T -> forall v, VR (open_ty_rec j U T) benv rho v ↔ VR T (benv++[VR U benv rho]) rho v. With j=0, benv=[]: open_ty_rec 0 U T = open_ty T U ✓ (definition open_ty); benv++[VR U [] rho] = [VR U [] rho] ✓. lc_ty_at (S 0) T = lc_ty_at 1 T ✓ HlcT.
So `apply (VR_open_ty_rec_subst T 0 [] rho U eq_refl HU0 HlcT v')` where HU0 : locally_closed_ty U = lc_ty_at 0 U from wf_ty_lc HwfU.
The lemma's conclusion is an iff with RHS VR T ([]++[VR U [] rho]) rho v'; that's definitionally VR T [VR U [] rho] rho v' ✓.

Then:
```
    assert (HtrOpen : TR (open_ty T U) [] rho (tm_tapp v (ty_subst_env sigma U))).
    { apply (proj2 (TR_congr (open_ty T U) T [] [VR U [] rho] rho rho (tm_tapp v (ty_subst_env sigma U)) (fun v' => Hpt v'))).
      exact HtrT. }
```
Wait TR_congr T1 T2 ... : (forall v, VR T1 ... ↔ VR T2 ...) -> (TR T1 ↔ TR T2). Here T1 = open_ty T U, T2 = T. Hpt : VR (open) [] ↔ VR T [P] ✓. proj2 : TR T2 (T) -> TR T1 (open) ✓. Goal TR (open) ...; apply proj2 leaves goal TR T ...; exact HtrT ✓.

T_TAbs:
Goal: TR (Ty_All T) [] rho (tm_tabs (apply_env σ γ t)).
```
  - (* T_TAbs *)
    simpl.
    change (TR (Ty_All T) [] rho (tm_tabs (apply_env sigma gamma t))).
    (* lc of the abstraction *)
    pose (X := fresh (L ++ fv_ty T ++ fv_ty t ++ fv_context Gamma)).
```
Wait: fv_ty t? t is a tm; type atoms of a term = fv_tm_ty t. fv_ty is for types. So use fv_tm_ty t. The freshness list: L ++ fv_ty T ++ fv_tm_ty t ++ fv_context Gamma.
We need X ∉ L, X ∉ fv_ty T, X ∉ fv_tm_ty t, X ∉ fv_context Gamma.
Define helper lemmas to project from fresh_not_in:
```
assert (HXL : ~ In X L). { subst X; intro Hin; apply (fresh_not_in (...)); apply in_or_app; ... }
```
The list is L ++ fv_ty T ++ fv_tm_ty t ++ fv_context Gamma. In Coq, `L ++ fv_ty T ++ fv_tm_ty t ++ fv_context Gamma` parses as ((L ++ fv_ty T) ++ fv_tm_ty t) ++ fv_context Gamma (left assoc). Membership projections: use `in_or_app` repeatedly. Let's define a helper tactic or lemma `In_app_inv`? To keep it simple, define each:
- X ∉ L: intro H; apply fresh_not_in; apply in_or_app; left; apply in_or_app; left; apply in_or_app; left; exact H.
  With left-assoc: (L ++ A) ++ B) ++ C. In x (((L++A)++B)++C) ↔ In x ((L++A)++B) \/ In x C; ↔ (In x (L++A) \/ In x B) \/ In x C; ↔ ((In x L \/ In x A) \/ In x B) \/ In x C.
  So X ∈ L → in_or_app left (in_or_app left (in_or_app left H)).
- fv_ty T: in_or_app left (in_or_app left (in_or_app right H)).
- fv_tm_ty t: in_or_app left (in_or_app right H).
- fv_context Gamma: in_or_app right H.
Alternatively use a list concatenation and a simpler freshness: choose X = fresh (L ++ fv_ty T ++ fv_tm_ty t ++ fv_context Gamma) and prove each by `intro Hin; apply (fresh_not_in _); apply in_or_app; ...`. We can write a small tactic:
```
assert (HXall : ~ In X (L ++ fv_ty T ++ fv_tm_ty t ++ fv_context Gamma)) by (subst X; apply fresh_not_in).
```
